Required unless `stop` is true.","type":"boolean"}},"additionalProperties":false},"eager_input_streaming":true}},{"name":"SendFeedback","description":"Use this tool to draft feedback about Claude Code when you hit a high-signal moment. That includes both PRODUCT issues and MODEL-BEHAVIOR issues:\n- a reproducible tool or product failure was just resolved or abandoned\n- the user clearly expressed frustration with Claude Code or with how you handled the task\n- you hit a missing capability that blocked a reasonable request\n- you notice, or the user points out, that your own behavior in this session went wrong, for example: you gave a confident answer then had to retract it; you stopped short and handed work back when you could have finished; you declined or disputed a reasonable request; you spawned more subagents than the task warranted; your tone was off; you asked more clarifying questions than needed; you expanded scope beyond what was asked\n\nThe draft is QUEUED LOCALLY. It is never sent without the user's explicit approval, and calling this tool renders no UI and does not interrupt the conversation, so never announce it or ask the user about it mid-task.\n\nWrite `details` as short labeled bullets in this exact order, one to three lines each, no narrative paragraphs:\n- **What happened:** the observed behavior vs. what was expected, with exact error text if short. Facts only.\n- **What the user said:** the user's own words that prompted this, quoted. If nothing did, write \"User didn't comment; observed by the model.\" Never paraphrase sentiment into a stronger claim.\n- **Repro:** the minimal steps or shape that reproduces it.\n- **Evidence:** identifiers a reader can chase, such as request IDs, timestamps, file paths, versions. Omit the bullet if there are none.\n\nConstraints:\n- Never fabricate or exaggerate user sentiment; report only what actually happened.\n- Everything in the draft must be sourced from the user or the session, never inferred: leave unknown fields blank rather than guess, and add a final **Cause:** bullet only for a root cause you verified in-session.\n- Use `area` to name the part of Claude Code the feedback is about (a feature, command, or workflow, e.g. \"hooks config\", \"/help\", \"file editing\") when there is a clear one; leave it blank otherwise.\n- Use `failure_mode` ONLY when the report is about model behavior (how Claude responded), not a product bug. Pick the single closest value, or `other` when it is a model-behavior issue that fits no listed value; omit the field only when the report is a product/tool bug with no model-behavior component.\n- Use `task_category` to name what kind of task the session was doing, or `other` when it is a clear task that fits no listed value. Omit only if genuinely unclear.\n- Do not include secrets or credentials. Refer to people by role (\"a teammate\", \"the PR reviewer\"), never by name, email address, or chat/user ID. This applies inside quoted user words too: replace a name or handle with a bracketed role (e.g. \"[a teammate]\") and keep the rest verbatim. Do not include customer-facing channel or DM IDs, or excerpts of customer content. Session, request, and run IDs, timestamps, repo/PR numbers, and file paths (written relative to the working directory, or ~-prefixed, not absolute paths under the user's home) remain the right evidence.\n- If the issue looks like a security vulnerability: describe the class of problem, never a working exploit or step-by-step extraction path.\n- Draft only at the natural moments listed above, and at most one draft per distinct issue; never re-draft the same issue in a session.","schema":{"name":"SendFeedback","description":"Use this tool to draft feedback about Claude Code when you hit a high-signal moment. Omit the bullet if there are none.\n\nConstraints:\n- Never fabricate or exaggerate user sentiment; report only what actually happened.\n- Everything in the draft must be sourced from the user or the session, never inferred: leave unknown fields blank rather than guess, and add a final **Cause:** bullet only for a root cause you verified in-session.\n- Use `area` to name the part of Claude Code the feedback is about (a feature, command, or workflow, e.g. \"hooks config\", \"/help\", \"file editing\") when there is a clear one; leave it blank otherwise.\n- Use `failure_mode` ONLY when the report is about model behavior (how Claude responded), not a product bug. Pick the single closest value, or `other` when it is a model-behavior issue that fits no listed value; omit the field only when the report is a product/tool bug with no model-behavior component.\n- Use `task_category` to name what kind of task the session was doing, or `other` when it is a clear task that fits no listed value. Omit only when the report is a product/tool bug with no model-behavior component.","type":"string","enum":["instruction_following","destructive_actions","code_quality","repetition_and_looping","model_regression","overconfidence_and_hallucination","context_and_memory","overeager","over_correction","stopping_short","dispute_or_decline","subagent_overspawn","tone_or_preachiness","excessive_questions","unwanted_scope","other"]},"task_category":{"description":"What kind of task the session was doing when the issue occurred, or `other` when it is a clear task that fits no listed value. Omit only if genuinely unclear.","type":"string","enum":["code_edit","debug","explain","plan","shell","search","review","other"]}},"required":["type","title","details"],"additionalProperties":false},"eager_input_streaming":true}},{"name":"Skill","description":"Invoke a skill.\n\nA skill is a packaged set of instructions the user or project has set up for a particular kind of task (deploy steps, a review checklist, a repo-specific workflow). Available skills appear in a system-reminder listing with one-line descriptions. When the task at hand is one a listed skill covers, call this tool first — the skill's instructions load into the turn for you to follow in place of your default approach; some skills instead run in a subagent and return the finished result. A skill that runs in the background returns only the agent's name — its result arrives later as a task notification, so don't wait on it or invoke it again in the meantime. Users may also ask for one by name (`/<name>`, or \"slash command\"); that's a request to invoke it.\n\n- `skill`: exact name from the listing, no leading slash. Plugin skills use `plugin:skill`. Directory-scoped skills are listed with a path prefix (`apps/web:deploy`); when both scoped and unscoped variants of a name exist, pick the one whose directory contains the files you're working on (most specific wins; unscoped otherwise).\n- `args`: optional arguments to pass through.\n\nOnly names from the listing (or that the user typed explicitly) are valid. Built-in CLI commands (`/help`, `/clear`, …) aren't skills. A skill that runs in the background returns only the agent's name — its result arrives later as a task notification, so don't wait on it or invoke it again in the meantime. Users may also ask for one by name (`/<name>`, or \"slash command\"); that's a request to invoke it.\n\n- `skill`: exact name from the listing, no leading slash. Plugin skills use `plugin:skill`. Directory-scoped skills are listed with a path prefix (`apps/web:deploy`); when both scoped and unscoped variants of a name exist, pick the one whose directory contains the files you're working on (most specific wins; unscoped otherwise).\n- `args`: optional arguments to pass through.\n\nOnly names from the listing (or that the user typed explicitly) are valid. Until fetched, only the name is known — there is no parameter schema, so the tool cannot be invoked. This tool takes a query, matches it against the deferred tool list, and returns the matched tools' complete JSONSchema definitions inside a <functions> block. Once a tool's schema appears in that result, it is callable exactly like any tool defined at the top of the prompt.\n\nResult format: each matched tool appears as one <function>{\"description\": \"...\", \"name\": \"...\", \"parameters\": {...}}</function> line inside the <functions> block — the same encoding as the tool list at the top of this prompt.\n\nQuery forms:\n- \"select:Read,Edit,Grep\" — fetch these exact tools by name\n- \"notebook jupyter\" — keyword search, up to max_results best matches\n- \"+slack send\" — require \"slack\" in the name, rank by remaining terms","schema":{"name":"ToolSearch","description":"Fetches full schema definitions for deferred tools so they can be called.\n\nDeferred tools appear by name in <system-reminder> messages. Until fetched, only the name is known — there is no parameter schema, so the tool cannot be invoked. This tool takes a query, matches it against the deferred tool list, and returns the matched tools' complete JSONSchema definitions inside a <functions> block. echo \"----\"; gh issue view 1; echo \"----\"; cat CONTEXT.md; ls docs docs/adr docs/agents","description":"Read T03 ticket, spec, and glossary"}},"wireIngestContext":{"toolu_01QGq14JMQ3LtuUsisGPY4rJ":{"cwd":"/Users/davelleat/code/iphs400-mp2-cms"}},"serverClassifierRequest":"bd46a803-6d51-428b-bc76-e1a3dc4af473","apiBlockIndex":0,"requestId":"req_011CfWb6KhUHTLvVh9P6aKY9","attributionSkill":"mattpocock-skills:implement","attributionPlugin":"mattpocock-skills","type":"assistant","uuid":"dd667714-940a-488e-9584-fcde16ea5d4d","timestamp":"2026-09-28T21:59:44.922Z","advisorModel":"claude-opus-5-5","effort":"high","perTurnEffort":"high","session_id":"999d2722-6532-412d-b424-27cb5954f6c3","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/davelleat/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"13282ec6-c552-4117-af56-20cf1c33be70","version":"2.1.283","gitBranch":"main"}
{"parentUuid":"dd667714-940a-488e-9584-fcde16ea5d4d","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"62b1f1f9-eaa0-4403-8c32-994cf2bcd569","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01QGq14JMQ3LtuUsisGPY4rJ","type":"tool_result","content":"----\ntitle:\tSpec: Kenyon IR Office CMS\nstate:\tOPEN\nauthor:\tDavelleAT (Davelle Ampofo-Twumasi)\nlabels:\tspec\ncomments:\t1\nassignees:\t\nprojects:\t\nmilestone:\t\nissue-type:\t\nparent:\t\nsub-issues:\t\nsub-issues-completed:\t\nblocked-by:\t\nblocking:\t\nnumber:\t1\n--\n## Problem Statement\n\nKenyon's Office of Institutional Research (Director + rotating student Analysts) currently publishes Data Bites and Reports by emailing files around and dropping them in a shared OneDrive folder. With multiple interns working at once, nobody can tell which version is current, and people outside the office resort to emailing IR directly to ask for the latest Factbook instead of finding it themselves.\n\n## Solution\n\nA CMS with two roles — Director (admin) and Analyst (editor; each intern gets their own named account) — that separates draft from published content. Analysts can draft, edit, publish, and unpublish Data Bites (short, dated Markdown items) without waiting on the Director. Reports (Factbook, Common Data Set, Survey Policy) are Markdown pages, same as Data Bites, with an optional attached file (e.g. the actual Factbook PDF, when one exists) — matching how these documents actually circulate while keeping the core rubric's required Markdown-body field intact. Only the Director can publish, edit, unpublish, or delete a Report once it exists. `cms publish` exports only published content to a static public site on GitHub Pages, with content-type-namespaced paths.\n\n## User Stories\n\n1. As a Director, I want to log in with a password, so that I can access the admin console.\n2. As an Analyst, I want to log in with my own account, so that whichever intern is on shift can post routine content under their own name.\n3. As anyone, I want a wrong password rejected with a generic message, so that the login form doesn't reveal whether an account exists.\n4. As any authenticated user (Director or Analyst), I want to log out, so that a shared machine doesn't stay signed in.\n5. As a Director, I want to create a new user account (Director or Analyst), so that I can grant access as staffing changes.\n6. As a Director, I want to change a user's role, so that I can correct access if someone's responsibilities change.\n7. As a Director, I want to deactivate a user, so that someone who's left the office can't log in anymore — even with a browser tab still holding an old session cookie — without deleting their history.\n8. As an Analyst, I want the admin console to hide user management entirely, so that I'm not tempted or able to touch accounts.\n9. As an Analyst, I want to create a Data Bite (title, slug, Markdown body), so that I can post a survey result or announcement.\n10. As an Analyst, I want to save a Data Bite as a draft, so that I can work on it across multiple sessions before it's ready.\n11. As an Analyst, I want to publish a Data Bite myself, so that I don't have to wait on the Director for routine content.\n12. As an Analyst, I want to edit or unpublish a Data Bite (including one another Analyst drafted or published), so that shift changes don't block fixing a mistake.\n13. As a Director, I want to delete any Data Bite, so that outdated or wrong content can be fully removed; Analysts cannot delete Data Bites.\n14. As an Analyst, I want to create a **draft** Report (title, slug, Markdown body) and optionally attach a file (e.g. the Factbook PDF, when an official document exists), so that I can prepare the next cited document.\n15. As an Analyst, I want to edit a draft Report — its Markdown body, or the attached file — including one another Analyst started, so that shift changes don't block prep work.\n16. As an Analyst, I want to be unable to publish, edit, unpublish, or delete a Report once it's published, so that cited documents can't be changed or taken down without Director review.\n17. As a Director, I want to publish, edit (Markdown body or the attached file), or unpublish any Report, so that I control what's cited externally.\n18. As a Director, I want to delete any Report, so that outdated cited documents can be fully removed.\n19. As a Director or Analyst, I want a dashboard showing counts of Data Bites and Reports by status, so that I can see the state of the office's content at a glance.\n20. As a Director or Analyst, I want to filter the content list by type (Data Bite/Report) and status (draft/published), so that I can find something quickly.\n21. As a Director or Analyst, I want a Markdown preview while editing a Data Bite or a Report's caption, so that I can see how it will actually render (post-sanitization) before publishing.\n22. As a visitor, I want to see only published Data Bites and Reports on the public site, so that I never see draft or unreleased data, and never a draft's uploaded file.\n23. As a visitor, I want published Reports to appear in the public site's navigation, so that I can find the Factbook or Common Data Set without searching.\n24. As a visitor, I want to read a published Report's Markdown body on its page, and download its attached file if one exists, so that I get the full content plus the official document when available.\n25. As a Director, I want to run `cms publish` and have it regenerate `site/` from only published content, writing Data Bites to `data-bites/<slug>.html`, Reports to `reports/<slug>.html`, and Report files to `reports/files/<slug>.<ext>`, all with relative paths, so that the export works correctly on GitHub Pages and nothing collides.\n26. As an Analyst or Director, I want a duplicate slug within the same content type rejected at save time with a clear error, so that two items can never overwrite each other's export path.\n27. As an uploader, I want a Report's optional attached file restricted to an allowlisted file type (PDF) and a size cap, so that the upload path can't be used to smuggle arbitrary or oversized files.\n28. As anyone, I want content, files, and sessions to survive a server restart, so that nothing is lost between work sessions.\n29. As a security-conscious Director, I want passwords stored as argon2 hashes and every state-changing form — including login and logout — protected by a CSRF token, so that the office's accounts and content can't be trivially compromised.\n30. As a security-conscious Director, I want Markdown (Data Bite bodies, Report captions) stored raw and sanitized only at render time (preview and export), so that a pasted `<script>` tag can never execute anywhere it's displayed.\n\n## Implementation Decisions\n\n- **Roles**: internal role values stay `admin` and `editor` (compatibility with `tests/conftest.py`'s `DEMO_USERS` and `client_as` fixture); templates display them as \"Director\" and \"Analyst\" respectively. No third role.\n- **DataBite**: title, slug, raw Markdown body, status (`draft`/`published`), author reference, `created_at`/`updated_at`.\n- **Report**: title, slug, required raw Markdown body (same shape as `DataBite`, satisfies core rubric C4), optional attached file (PDF; allowlisted extension + MIME type; size-capped — exact cap is an implementation detail, pick something generous but bounded, e.g. 20MB), status, author reference, `created_at`/`updated_at`. The optional-file part doubles as the \"Media uploads\" stretch goal.\n- **Shift-continuity editing**: any Analyst may edit an item (Data Bite, or a Report while still draft) regardless of who created or last edited it; the original author and `created_at` are preserved, only `updated_at` changes.\n- **Permission rule** (ADR-003): Analysts — full create/edit/publish/unpublish on `DataBite`, no delete. Analysts — create/edit on **draft** `Report` only (Markdown body and/or the optional attached file); once a `Report` is published, only the Director may edit, unpublish, or delete it, and only the Director may ever publish one. Directors — full CRUD + publish on both, plus user management. Enforced server-side on every route — hiding a link is not access control (Security Checklist item 10).\n- **Auth**: every `/admin/*` route requires an authenticated session (redirect to login if anonymous). Every protected request re-validates the session's user as active server-side — a deactivated user's existing signed cookie must fail on the next request, not just on next login. Session via signed cookies (`itsdangerous`), password hashing via `argon2-cffi`.\n- **CSRF**: a per-session token embedded in every state-changing form and validated server-side on POST, including `/login` and `/logout`.\n- **Markdown/sanitization pipeline**: raw Markdown is what's stored (Data Bite bodies, Report captions). Rendering (preview or export) always runs `markdown-it-py` → `nh3` before display; pre-rendered/sanitized HTML is never persisted.\n- **File uploads (optional)**: handled via the existing `python-multipart` dependency (no new dependency needed). When present, the file is stored outside `site/` (e.g. a local `uploads/` directory, gitignored like the DB) until publish; a draft Report's file must never appear in `site/`, same rule as draft content generally. A Report with no attached file exports as a plain Markdown page, same as a Data Bite.\n- **Publish gate & export paths**: an item reaches `site/` only when `status == published`. `cms publish` (`app/publish.py`) writes published `DataBite` rows to `data-bites/<slug>.html`, published `Report` rows to `reports/<slug>.html` (rendering the Markdown body, plus a download link when a file is attached), and copies each published Report's attached file, if any, to `reports/files/<slug>.<ext>` — all relative paths. Namespacing by type means a Data Bite and a Report may share a slug; two items of the *same* type sharing a slug is rejected at save time with a form validation error.\n- **Navigation**: public nav is built from published `Report`s only.\n- **Individual Analyst accounts** (ADR-003, reversed from an earlier shared-account draft): each intern gets their own named Analyst-role account, created and deactivated by the Director as rotations change. Shift continuity (any Analyst can edit any other Analyst's draft) is preserved as a permission rule, not by sharing a login — author and created_at are unaffected by who edits.\n\n## Testing Decisions\n\n- All tests exercise real HTTP behavior through `TestClient` via the existing `client` / `client_as(role)` fixtures in `tests/conftest.py` — no isolated unit-testing of permission logic. `DEMO_USERS`/`client_as` keeps using `\"admin\"`/`\"editor\"` role keys (per the internal-role-values decision above); UI-label tests separately assert \"Director\"/\"Analyst\" appear in rendered admin templates.\n- Prior art: `tests/test_t00.py` — status-code and text-content assertions against real requests, extended with DB-state and exported-file assertions where relevant.\n- Access-control tests per route: every Director-only route rejects an Analyst session (403/redirect) and rejects an anonymous session (redirect to login) — Security Checklist items 4–5, rubric D3. Includes a specific test that a deactivated user's still-valid-looking session cookie is rejected on the next request.\n- CSRF tests: replay one POST per state-changing form (including login and logout) with the token stripped, expect rejection — Security Checklist item 3, rubric D2.\n- Sanitization test: save a `<script>`/`onerror=` payload in a Data Bite body and a Report body, publish, assert it's inert in both the admin preview and the exported HTML — Security Checklist item 7, rubric D4.\n- Draft-leak test: publish with a draft Data Bite and a draft Report (with its file) present, assert both are absent from `site/` — Security Checklist item 8.\n- Upload-validation test: a non-PDF or oversized attached file is rejected at upload time with a clear error; a Report with no attached file still publishes and exports correctly.\n- Slug-collision test: saving a second item of the same content type with an existing slug is rejected with a validation error, not a silent overwrite.\n- Published-Report lockdown test: an Analyst attempting to edit (Markdown body or attached file), unpublish, or delete an already-published Report is rejected, even though they could when it was a draft.\n\n## Out of Scope\n\nPer the manual and rubric: multi-site support, drag-and-drop page builders, rich-text WYSIWYG editors. Also out of scope for the core spec (available separately as `stretch` tickets if pursued): categories/tags, revision history/rollback, scheduled publishing, site search, comments/moderation, a plugin/hook system, switchable themes, per-intern individual accounts (deliberately rejected, see ADR-003), uploads for Data Bites (Data Bites stay Markdown-only; only Reports may optionally carry an attached file).\n\n## Further Notes\n\nThis spec covers all 7 required capabilities from the rubric, plus the \"Media uploads\" stretch goal folded into the Report content type. `/to-tickets` will slice this into 7–9 vertical slices, each touching data model + route + template + test per rubric B3.\n\n\n----\n# Kenyon Office of Institutional Research (IR) CMS\n\nA CMS for Kenyon College's Office of Institutional Research: a two-person office\n(Director + Analyst/intern staff) that publishes survey results, data briefs,\nand durable reference documents like the Factbook and Common Data Set.\n\n## Language\n\n**Director**:\nThe admin role. Modeled on the real office's Associate VP for Institutional\nResearch — the only person who can publish or edit a Report, manage user\naccounts, and deactivate access.\n_Avoid_: Admin, superuser\n\n**Analyst**:\nThe editor role. Each intern gets their own named Analyst account (see\nADR-003). Can draft and publish Data Bites directly; can draft but not\npublish or edit Reports. Any Analyst may edit any other Analyst's draft\n(shift continuity), though the original author is preserved.\n_Avoid_: Editor, intern (interns are Analysts, not a separate role), staff\n\n**Data Bite**:\nA Post: a short, dated item — a survey result, an enrollment snapshot, an\nannouncement. The frequent, low-stakes content type. Analysts can publish\nthese without Director approval.\n_Avoid_: Update, post, announcement, article\n\n**Report**:\nA Page: a durable, cite-able reference document — the Factbook, a Common Data\nSet, the Survey Coordination Policy. Appears in public navigation. Only the\nDirector can publish or edit a Report, even one an Analyst drafted.\n_Avoid_: Page, document\n\n**Draft**:\nContent (a Data Bite or Report) that has been saved but not published. Never\nappears on the public site. Used for unreleased data, e.g. enrollment numbers\nstill being verified before the official count.\n_Avoid_: Unpublished, pending\n\n**Published**:\nContent visible on the public site. The one-way gate: a Report only crosses\nit with Director action.\n_Avoid_: Live, public\n\n**Factbook**:\nA specific Report: the office's running reference document of admissions,\nenrollment, diversity, and academic program statistics.\n\n**Common Data Set (CDS)**:\nA specific Report: a standardized annual institutional data submission,\npublished publicly.\n\n**Survey Calendar**:\nA specific Report: the schedule of past, current, and upcoming surveys the\noffice runs (e.g. faculty/staff surveys, external review surveys).\ndocs:\nadr\nagents\nhandoff\nmanual_iphs400_mp2-web-cms_20260922.md\nmp2-grading-rubric_20260922.md\nmp2-setup_context-threshold-hook_20260922.md\nprocess\nscreenshots\ntranscripts\n\ndocs/adr:\nADR-001-local-admin-static-public.md\nADR-002-stack.md\nADR-003-split-publish-rights.md\n\ndocs/agents:\ndomain.md\nissue-tracker.md","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"8428e69f-b678-4928-ad31-99e04e158974","timestamp":"2026-09-28T21:59:47.371Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"----\ntitle:\tSpec: Kenyon IR Office CMS\nstate:\tOPEN\nauthor:\tDavelleAT (Davelle Ampofo-Twumasi)\nlabels:\tspec\ncomments:\t1\nassignees:\t\nprojects:\t\nmilestone:\t\nissue-type:\t\nparent:\t\nsub-issues:\t\nsub-issues-completed:\t\nblocked-by:\t\nblocking:\t\nnumber:\t1\n--\n## Problem Statement\n\nKenyon's Office of Institutional Research (Director + rotating student Analysts) currently publishes Data Bites and Reports by emailing files around and dropping them in a shared OneDrive folder. With multiple interns working at once, nobody can tell which version is current, and people outside the office resort to emailing IR directly to ask for the latest Factbook instead of finding it themselves.\n\n## Solution\n\nA CMS with two roles — Director (admin) and Analyst (editor; each intern gets their own named account) — that separates draft from published content. Analysts can draft, edit, publish, and unpublish Data Bites (short, dated Markdown items) without waiting on the Director. Reports (Factbook, Common Data Set, Survey Policy) are Markdown pages, same as Data Bites, with an optional attached file (e.g. the actual Factbook PDF, when one exists) — matching how these documents actually circulate while keeping the core rubric's required Markdown-body field intact. Only the Director can publish, edit, unpublish, or delete a Report once it exists. `cms publish` exports only published content to a static public site on GitHub Pages, with content-type-namespaced paths.\n\n## User Stories\n\n1. As a Director, I want to log in with a password, so that I can access the admin console.\n2. As an Analyst, I want to log in with my own account, so that whichever intern is on shift can post routine content under their own name.\n3. As anyone, I want a wrong password rejected with a generic message, so that the login form doesn't reveal whether an account exists.\n4. As any authenticated user (Director or Analyst), I want to log out, so that a shared machine doesn't stay signed in.\n5. As a Director, I want to create a new user account (Director or Analyst), so that I can grant access as staffing changes.\n6. As a Director, I want to change a user's role, so that I can correct access if someone's responsibilities change.\n7. As a Director, I want to deactivate a user, so that someone who's left the office can't log in anymore — even with a browser tab still holding an old session cookie — without deleting their history.\n8. As an Analyst, I want the admin console to hide user management entirely, so that I'm not tempted or able to touch accounts.\n9. As an Analyst, I want to create a Data Bite (title, slug, Markdown body), so that I can post a survey result or announcement.\n10. As an Analyst, I want to save a Data Bite as a draft, so that I can work on it across multiple sessions before it's ready.\n11. As an Analyst, I want to publish a Data Bite myself, so that I don't have to wait on the Director for routine content.\n12. As an Analyst, I want to edit or unpublish a Data Bite (including one another Analyst drafted or published), so that shift changes don't block fixing a mistake.\n13. As a Director, I want to delete any Data Bite, so that outdated or wrong content can be fully removed; Analysts cannot delete Data Bites.\n14. As an Analyst, I want to create a **draft** Report (title, slug, Markdown body) and optionally attach a file (e.g. the Factbook PDF, when an official document exists), so that I can prepare the next cited document.\n15. As an Analyst, I want to edit a draft Report — its Markdown body, or the attached file — including one another Analyst started, so that shift changes don't block prep work.\n16. As an Analyst, I want to be unable to publish, edit, unpublish, or delete a Report once it's published, so that cited documents can't be changed or taken down without Director review.\n17. As a Director, I want to publish, edit (Markdown body or the attached file), or unpublish any Report, so that I control what's cited externally.\n18. As a Director, I want to delete any Report, so that outdated cited documents can be fully removed.\n19. As a Director or Analyst, I want a dashboard showing counts of Data Bites and Reports by status, so that I can see the state of the office's content at a glance.\n20. As a Director or Analyst, I want to filter the content list by type (Data Bite/Report) and status (draft/published), so that I can find something quickly.\n21. As a Director or Analyst, I want a Markdown preview while editing a Data Bite or a Report's caption, so that I can see how it will actually render (post-sanitization) before publishing.\n22. As a visitor, I want to see only published Data Bites and Reports on the public site, so that I never see draft or unreleased data, and never a draft's uploaded file.\n23. As a visitor, I want published Reports to appear in the public site's navigation, so that I can find the Factbook or Common Data Set without searching.\n24. As a visitor, I want to read a published Report's Markdown body on its page, and download its attached file if one exists, so that I get the full content plus the official document when available.\n25. As a Director, I want to run `cms publish` and have it regenerate `site/` from only published content, writing Data Bites to `data-bites/<slug>.html`, Reports to `reports/<slug>.html`, and Report files to `reports/files/<slug>.<ext>`, all with relative paths, so that the export works correctly on GitHub Pages and nothing collides.\n26. As an Analyst or Director, I want a duplicate slug within the same content type rejected at save time with a clear error, so that two items can never overwrite each other's export path.\n27. As an uploader, I want a Report's optional attached file restricted to an allowlisted file type (PDF) and a size cap, so that the upload path can't be used to smuggle arbitrary or oversized files.\n28. As anyone, I want content, files, and sessions to survive a server restart, so that nothing is lost between work sessions.\n29. As a security-conscious Director, I want passwords stored as argon2 hashes and every state-changing form — including login and logout — protected by a CSRF token, so that the office's accounts and content can't be trivially compromised.\n30. As a security-conscious Director, I want Markdown (Data Bite bodies, Report captions) stored raw and sanitized only at render time (preview and export), so that a pasted `<script>` tag can never execute anywhere it's displayed.\n\n## Implementation Decisions\n\n- **Roles**: internal role values stay `admin` and `editor` (compatibility with `tests/conftest.py`'s `DEMO_USERS` and `client_as` fixture); templates display them as \"Director\" and \"Analyst\" respectively. No third role.\n- **DataBite**: title, slug, raw Markdown body, status (`draft`/`published`), author reference, `created_at`/`updated_at`.\n- **Report**: title, slug, required raw Markdown body (same shape as `DataBite`, satisfies core rubric C4), optional attached file (PDF; allowlisted extension + MIME type; size-capped — exact cap is an implementation detail, pick something generous but bounded, e.g. 20MB), status, author reference, `created_at`/`updated_at`. The optional-file part doubles as the \"Media uploads\" stretch goal.\n- **Shift-continuity editing**: any Analyst may edit an item (Data Bite, or a Report while still draft) regardless of who created or last edited it; the original author and `created_at` are preserved, only `updated_at` changes.\n- **Permission rule** (ADR-003): Analysts — full create/edit/publish/unpublish on `DataBite`, no delete. Analysts — create/edit on **draft** `Report` only (Markdown body and/or the optional attached file); once a `Report` is published, only the Director may edit, unpublish, or delete it, and only the Director may ever publish one. Directors — full CRUD + publish on both, plus user management. Enforced server-side on every route — hiding a link is not access control (Security Checklist item 10).\n- **Auth**: every `/admin/*` route requires an authenticated session (redirect to login if anonymous). Every protected request re-validates the session's user as active server-side — a deactivated user's existing signed cookie must fail on the next request, not just on next login. Session via signed cookies (`itsdangerous`), password hashing via `argon2-cffi`.\n- **CSRF**: a per-session token embedded in every state-changing form and validated server-side on POST, including `/login` and `/logout`.\n- **Markdown/sanitization pipeline**: raw Markdown is what's stored (Data Bite bodies, Report captions). Rendering (preview or export) always runs `markdown-it-py` → `nh3` before display; pre-rendered/sanitized HTML is never persisted.\n- **File uploads (optional)**: handled via the existing `python-multipart` dependency (no new dependency needed). When present, the file is stored outside `site/` (e.g. a local `uploads/` directory, gitignored like the DB) until publish; a draft Report's file must never appear in `site/`, same rule as draft content generally. A Report with no attached file exports as a plain Markdown page, same as a Data Bite.\n- **Publish gate & export paths**: an item reaches `site/` only when `status == published`. `cms publish` (`app/publish.py`) writes published `DataBite` rows to `data-bites/<slug>.html`, published `Report` rows to `reports/<slug>.html` (rendering the Markdown body, plus a download link when a file is attached), and copies each published Report's attached file, if any, to `reports/files/<slug>.<ext>` — all relative paths. Namespacing by type means a Data Bite and a Report may share a slug; two items of the *same* type sharing a slug is rejected at save time with a form validation error.\n- **Navigation**: public nav is built from published `Report`s only.\n- **Individual Analyst accounts** (ADR-003, reversed from an earlier shared-account draft): each intern gets their own named Analyst-role account, created and deactivated by the Director as rotations change. Shift continuity (any Analyst can edit any other Analyst's draft) is preserved as a permission rule, not by sharing a login — author and created_at are unaffected by who edits.\n\n## Testing Decisions\n\n- All tests exercise real HTTP behavior through `TestClient` via the existing `client` / `client_as(role)` fixtures in `tests/conftest.py` — no isolated unit-testing of permission logic. `DEMO_USERS`/`client_as` keeps using `\"admin\"`/`\"editor\"` role keys (per the internal-role-values decision above); UI-label tests separately assert \"Director\"/\"Analyst\" appear in rendered admin templates.\n- Prior art: `tests/test_t00.py` — status-code and text-content assertions against real requests, extended with DB-state and exported-file assertions where relevant.\n- Access-control tests per route: every Director-only route rejects an Analyst session (403/redirect) and rejects an anonymous session (redirect to login) — Security Checklist items 4–5, rubric D3. Includes a specific test that a deactivated user's still-valid-looking session cookie is rejected on the next request.\n- CSRF tests: replay one POST per state-changing form (including login and logout) with the token stripped, expect rejection — Security Checklist item 3, rubric D2.\n- Sanitization test: save a `<script>`/`onerror=` payload in a Data Bite body and a Report body, publish, assert it's inert in both the admin preview and the exported HTML — Security Checklist item 7, rubric D4.\n- Draft-leak test: publish with a draft Data Bite and a draft Report (with its file) present, assert both are absent from `site/` — Security Checklist item 8.\n- Upload-validation test: a non-PDF or oversized attached file is rejected at upload time with a clear error; a Report with no attached file still publishes and exports correctly.\n- Slug-collision test: saving a second item of the same content type with an existing slug is rejected with a validation error, not a silent overwrite.\n- Published-Report lockdown test: an Analyst attempting to edit (Markdown body or attached file), unpublish, or delete an already-published Report is rejected, even though they could when it was a draft.\n\n## Out of Scope\n\nPer the manual and rubric: multi-site support, drag-and-drop page builders, rich-text WYSIWYG editors. Also out of scope for the core spec (available separately as `stretch` tickets if pursued): categories/tags, revision history/rollback, scheduled publishing, site search, comments/moderation, a plugin/hook system, switchable themes, per-intern individual accounts (deliberately rejected, see ADR-003), uploads for Data Bites (Data Bites stay Markdown-only; only Reports may optionally carry an attached file).\n\n## Further Notes\n\nThis spec covers all 7 required capabilities from the rubric, plus the \"Media uploads\" stretch goal folded into the Report content type. `/to-tickets` will slice this into 7–9 vertical slices, each touching data model + route + template + test per rubric B3.\n\n\n----\n# Kenyon Office of Institutional Research (IR) CMS\n\nA CMS for Kenyon College's Office of Institutional Research: a two-person office\n(Director + Analyst/intern staff) that publishes survey results, data briefs,\nand durable reference documents like the Factbook and Common Data Set.\n\n## Language\n\n**Director**:\nThe admin role. Any Analyst may edit any other Analyst's draft\n(shift continuity), though the original author is preserved.\n_Avoid_: Editor, intern (interns are Analysts, not a separate role), staff\n\n**Data Bite**:\nA Post: a short, dated item — a survey result, an enrollment snapshot, an\nannouncement. The frequent, low-stakes content type. Analysts can publish\nthese without Director approval.\n_Avoid_: Update, post, announcement, article\n\n**Report**:\nA Page: a durable, cite-able reference document — the Factbook, a Common Data\nSet, the Survey Coordination Policy. Appears in public navigation. Only the\nDirector can publish or edit a Report, even one an Analyst drafted.\n_Avoid_: Page, document\n\n**Draft**:\nContent (a Data Bite or Report) that has been saved but not published. Never\nappears on the public site. Used for unreleased data, e.g. enrollment numbers\nstill being verified before the official count.\n_Avoid_: Unpublished, pending\n\n**Published**:\nContent visible on the public site. The one-way gate: a Report only crosses\nit with Director action.\n_Avoid_: Live, public\n\n**Factbook**:\nA specific Report: the office's running reference document of admissions,\nenrollment, specific Report: the schedule of past, current, and upcoming surveys the\noffice runs (e.g. faculty/staff surveys, external review surveys).\ndocs:\nadr\nagents\nhandoff\nmanual_iphs400_mp2-web-cms_20260922.md\nmp2-grading-rubric_20260922.md\nmp2-setup_context-threshold-hook_20260922.md\nprocess\nscreenshots\ntranscripts\n\ndocs/adr:\nADR-001-local-admin-static-public.md\nADR-002-stack.md\nADR-003-split-publish-rights.md\n\ndocs/agents:\ndomain.md\nissue-tracker.md","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"serverClassifierContext":{"request":"bd46a803-6d51-428b-bc76-e1a3dc4af473","context":{"git_state":{"cwd":"/Users/davelleat/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","root":"/Users/davelleat/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","branch":"main","default_branch":"main","status":{"clean":false,"counts":{"staged":0,"modified":1,"untracked":null,"untracked_normal":1},"porcelain":null,"truncated":false},"visibility":{"origin":{"host":"github.com","remote":"DavelleAT/iphs400-mp2-cms","visibility":"public"},"push_remote":"origin","remotes":[{"name":"origin","host":"github.com","remote":"DavelleAT/iphs400-mp2-cms","visibility":"public"}],"visibility_cache":[{"host":"github.com","remote":"DavelleAT/iphs400-mp2-cms","visibility":"public"}]}},"live_cwd":"/Users/davelleat/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","platform":"macos"}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"dd667714-940a-488e-9584-fcde16ea5d4d","session_id":"999d2722-6532-412d-b424-27cb5954f6c3","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/davelleat/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"13282ec6-c552-4117-af56-20cf1c33be70","version":"2.1.283","gitBranch":"main"}

{"parentUuid":"2207fdeb-a80f-4e03-8dd4-e0d77ce549b9","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"62b1f1f9-eaa0-4403-8c32-994cf2bcd569","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_019XM94k6BqKUYNWUdnR25jN","type":"tool_result","content":"exit=0\nauthor:\tDavelleAT\nassociation:\towner\nedited:\ttrue\nstatus:\tnone\n--\n## /code-review — T02 (Standards + Spec axes)\n\nReviewed the T02 working tree against `d4d3707`. Scope: `app/`, `templates/`, `tests/`, `scripts/seed_demo.py`.\n\n### Standards\n\nNo hard violations of `CLAUDE.md` / `SECURITY-CHECKLIST.md`. Checked: CSRF on all three new POSTs, access control enforced by the router's `require_director` dependency (not the hidden link), argon2 unchanged, no plain password echoed on a failed create, no new dependency, Director/Analyst in user-facing text.\n\n| Finding | Resolution |\n|---|---|\n| **UI trap:** \"Change role\" / \"Deactivate\" controls were shown on the Director's own row but always failed with 400 | **Fixed.** Own row (marked \"(you)\") and deactivated rows show text, not controls. Server-side refusal stays; test asserts no `<form>` on your own row. |\n| `list_users()` ran `SELECT *`, so `password_hash` reached the template context | **Fixed.** Selects `id, email, name, role, is_active` only. |\n| `seed_demo.py` caught only `DuplicateEmail`; a `.env` password under 8 chars crashed with a traceback | **Fixed.** Any other `UserError` prints `CMS_…_PASSWORD: <message>` and exits 1. |\n| Duplicated Code: the self-lockout check was written two ways (one raised `UserError` inside a `try` as control flow) | **Fixed.** Both routes return the 400 page directly. |\n| Duplicated Code: template hardcoded `minlength=\"8\"` | **Fixed.** Uses `MIN_PASSWORD_LENGTH` from `app/users.py`. |\n| Duplicated Code: \"Choose a role: Director or Analyst.\" hardcoded the labels | **Fixed.** Built from `ROLE_LABELS`. |\n| Placement: `require_director` lived in `app/routes/users.py` | **Fixed.** Moved to `app/auth.py` next to `current_user`; T04 Report routes will need it. |\n| Test coupling: `test_t01_auth.py` imported private helpers from `test_t02_users.py` | **Fixed.** `post_form`, `user_row`, `user_id` now live in `tests/conftest.py` beside `csrf_from`. |\n| Mysterious Name: `_EMAIL` | **Fixed.** `_EMAIL_PATTERN`. |\n| Primitive Obsession: `role == \"admin\"` checked in `require_director` and `home.html` | **Kept.** Two sites; T01 kept roles as bare strings deliberately (spec: \"No third role\"). Revisit if T04 adds a third. |\n\n### Spec\n\nAll six acceptance criteria met, each with a route and tests (parametrized over every user-management route for Analyst 403 / anonymous redirect / missing CSRF).\n\n| Finding | Resolution |\n|---|---|\n| **Vacuous test:** `test_director_sees_every_user_with_role_labels` passed whatever the per-user role was, since each `<select>` renders both labels | **Fixed.** Now parametrized per role; asserts the *selected* option (or plain text on your own row) in that user's row. |\n| Scope creep: self-lockout guard (a Director can't demote/deactivate themselves) | **Kept.** Without it the only Director can lock the office out of user management with no UI recovery. It's the smallest rule that guarantees ≥1 active Director. |\n| Scope creep: create-user validation (email shape, non-blank name, password ≥ 8 chars) | **Kept.** Without it a duplicate email or unknown role was a 500. 8 is the NIST SP 800-63B minimum; change `MIN_PASSWORD_LENGTH` if the office wants longer. |\n| Scope creep: T01's deactivation tests now go through the T02 UI | **Kept.** Resolves the T01 review's deferred item (\"Deactivation moves to the T02 UI; revisit then\") and matches the spec's \"real HTTP behaviour\" testing decision. Helpers now shared via conftest, not cross-imported. |\n| No reactivate action; role form showed on deactivated rows | **Partly fixed.** Deactivated rows no longer show controls. Reactivation isn't asked for (story 7 only needs history kept, which it is); left out. |\n| **Spec text:** issue #1 \"Out of Scope\" still lists per-intern accounts as \"deliberately rejected\", contradicting Implementation Decisions / ADR-003 | **Not a code issue** (also raised in T01). Code follows ADR-003. Issue #1 needs a manual edit. |\n\n**Tests:** `tests/test_t02_users.py` (24) + T00/T01: all pass. Full suite: 53 passed; the 14 failures are the unfinished course exercises (`ctx_guard`, `usage_report`), same as baseline. Mutation-checked: removing the Director check turns 8 tests red; removing `require_csrf` turns the 3 CSRF tests red. Live server (curl, temp DB): anonymous → 303 /login, Analyst → 403, no-CSRF create → 403, short password → 400 with message, create → new user logs in, promote/demote takes effect on the Analyst's next request, self-deactivate → 400, unknown id → 404, deactivate → Analyst's open session redirected to login and re-login → 401.\n\n--","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"f40cc40e-9ebe-4953-884d-60f8d3a2c1da","timestamp":"2026-09-28T21:59:52.093Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"exit=0\nauthor:\tDavelleAT\nassociation:\towner\nedited:\ttrue\nstatus:\tnone\n--\n## /code-review — T02 (Standards + Spec axes)\n\nReviewed the T02 working tree against `d4d3707`. {"parentUuid":"7bf9255a-026e-4262-b33d-9fda5bd24e4d","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"62b1f1f9-eaa0-4403-8c32-994cf2bcd569","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01XsvCJv4rCRCjJUKv62X996","type":"tool_result","content":"T03: Data Bites\n## Parent\n\nPart of #1\n\n## What to build\n\nAnalyst can create, edit, publish, and unpublish a Data Bite; Director can additionally delete one. Drafts never leak into any public-facing query. Slug uniqueness is enforced within Data Bites. Any Analyst can edit any Data Bite (shift continuity) while the original author and created_at are preserved.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] Analyst can create a Data Bite (title, slug, Markdown body) as a draft\n- [ ] Analyst can publish and unpublish a Data Bite\n- [ ] Analyst cannot delete a Data Bite (403/redirect); Director can\n- [ ] A second Data Bite with a duplicate slug is rejected with a validation error\n- [ ] Editing a Data Bite preserves the original author and created_at; only updated_at changes\n- [ ] Any Analyst (not just the original author) can edit any Data Bite\n- [ ] A draft Data Bite is never returned by any public-facing query\n- [ ] All state-changing POSTs require CSRF; anonymous/Analyst access is checked where restricted\n- [ ] Tests cover all of the above\n\n## Blocked by\n\n- #2 (T01)","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"33b99106-7294-4e29-ab10-1db91fef240e","timestamp":"2026-09-28T22:00:28.661Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"T03: Data Bites\n## Parent\n\nPart of #1\n\n## What to build\n\nAnalyst can create, edit, publish, and unpublish a Data Bite; Director can additionally delete one. Drafts never leak into any public-facing query. Slug uniqueness is enforced within Data Bites. Any Analyst can edit any Data Bite (shift continuity) while the original author and created_at are preserved.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] Analyst can create a Data Bite (title, slug, Markdown body) as a draft\n- [ ] Analyst can publish and unpublish a Data Bite\n- [ ] Analyst cannot delete a Data Bite (403/redirect); Director can\n- [ ] A second Data Bite with a duplicate slug is rejected with a validation error\n- [ ] Editing a Data Bite preserves the original author and created_at; only updated_at changes\n- [ ] Any Analyst (not just the original author) can edit any Data Bite\n- [ ] A draft Data Bite is never returned by any public-facing query\n- [ ] All state-changing POSTs require CSRF; anonymous/Analyst access is checked where restricted\n- [ ] Tests cover all of the above\n\n## Blocked by\n\n- #2 (T01)","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"serverClassifierContext":{"request":"256c6e1b-bcbd-4e73-bc06-32d1700b313c","context":{"git_state":{"cwd":"/Users/davelleat/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","root":"/Users/davelleat/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","branch":"main","default_branch":"main","status":{"clean":false,"counts":{"staged":0,"modified":1,"untracked":null,"untracked_normal":1},"porcelain":null,"truncated":false},"visibility":{"origin":{"host":"github.com","remote":"DavelleAT/iphs400-mp2-cms","visibility":"public"},"push_remote":"origin","remotes":[{"name":"origin","host":"github.com","remote":"DavelleAT/iphs400-mp2-cms","visibility":"public"}],"visibility_cache":[{"host":"github.com","remote":"DavelleAT/iphs400-mp2-cms","visibility":"public"}]}},"live_cwd":"/Users/davelleat/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","platform":"macos"}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"7bf9255a-026e-4262-b33d-9fda5bd24e4d","session_id":"999d2722-6532-412d-b424-27cb5954f6c3","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/davelleat/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"13282ec6-c552-4117-af56-20cf1c33be70","version":"2.1.283","gitBranch":"main"}

{"parentUuid":"265c362d-9ba8-4f8f-bcf4-d0830da51dc1","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"62b1f1f9-eaa0-4403-8c32-994cf2bcd569","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_019L22tfuu6P4bHnVrhmeUxs","type":"tool_result","content":"=== app/db.py\n     1\t\"\"\"SQLite access. Every table's schema lives here, so init_db() builds them all.\"\"\"\n     2\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n     3\t\n     4\timport sqlite3\n     5\tfrom collections.abc import Iterator\n     6\tfrom contextlib import contextmanager\n     7\t\n     8\tfrom app import settings\n     9\t\n    10\tSCHEMA = \"\"\"\n    11\tCREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS users (\n    12\t    id            INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,\n    13\t    email         TEXT    NOT NULL UNIQUE COLLATE NOCASE,\n    14\t    name          TEXT    NOT NULL,\n    15\t    role          TEXT    NOT NULL CHECK (role IN ('admin', 'editor')),\n    16\t    password_hash TEXT    NOT NULL,\n    17\t    is_active     INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1,\n    18\t    created_at    TEXT    NOT NULL DEFAULT (datetime('now'))\n    19\t);\n    20\t\"\"\"\n    21\t\n    22\t\n    23\t@contextmanager\n    24\tdef connect() -> Iterator[sqlite3.Connection]:\n    25\t    \"\"\"Open the database named by settings (read at call time, so tests can swap\n    26\t    it). Commits on success, rolls back on error, always closes.\"\"\"\n    27\t    conn = sqlite3.connect(settings.DATABASE_PATH)\n    28\t    conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row\n    29\t    conn.execute(\"PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON\")\n    30\t    try:\n    31\t        with conn:\n    32\t            yield conn\n    33\t    finally:\n    34\t        conn.close()\n    35\t\n    36\t\n    37\tdef init_db() -> None:\n    38\t    with connect() as conn:\n    39\t        conn.executescript(SCHEMA)\n=== app/auth.py\n     1\t\"\"\"Sessions and CSRF.\n     2\t\n     3\tThe session is a signed cookie (Starlette's SessionMiddleware, which signs with\n     4\titsdangerous). It holds only the user's id and a CSRF token. The user is\n     5\tre-loaded from the database on every protected request, so deactivating an\n     6\taccount ends its sessions at once.\n     7\t\"\"\"\n     8\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n     9\t\n    10\timport secrets\n    11\timport sqlite3\n    12\t\n    13\tfrom fastapi import Depends, HTTPException, Request\n    14\t\n    15\tfrom app import users\n    16\t\n    17\t\n    18\tclass LoginRequired(Exception):\n    19\t    \"\"\"Raised by current_user; main.py turns it into a redirect to /login.\"\"\"\n    20\t\n    21\t\n    22\tdef current_user(request: Request) -> sqlite3.Row:\n    23\t    \"\"\"Dependency: the logged-in, still-active user, or LoginRequired.\"\"\"\n    24\t    user_id = request.session.get(\"user_id\")\n    25\t    user = users.get_active_user(user_id) if user_id is not None else None\n    26\t    if user is None:\n    27\t        request.session.clear()\n    28\t        raise LoginRequired\n    29\t    return user\n    30\t\n    31\t\n    32\tdef require_director(user: sqlite3.Row = Depends(current_user)) -> sqlite3.Row:\n    33\t    \"\"\"Dependency: current_user, but 403 unless they are a Director.\"\"\"\n    34\t    if user[\"role\"] != \"admin\":\n    35\t        raise HTTPException(status_code=403, detail=\"Directors only.\")\n    36\t    return user\n    37\t\n    38\t\n    39\tdef log_in(request: Request, user: sqlite3.Row) -> None:\n    40\t    # A fresh session (and CSRF token) on login, so a pre-login cookie planted\n    41\t    # by someone else never becomes an authenticated one.\n    42\t    request.session.clear()\n    43\t    request.session[\"user_id\"] = user[\"id\"]\n    44\t    csrf_token(request)\n    45\t\n    46\t\n    47\tdef log_out(request: Request) -> None:\n    48\t    request.session.clear()\n    49\t\n    50\t\n    51\tdef csrf_token(request: Request) -> str:\n    52\t    \"\"\"The session's CSRF token, created on first use. Templates call this.\"\"\"\n    53\t    token = request.session.get(\"csrf_token\")\n    54\t    if token is None:\n    55\t        token = request.session[\"csrf_token\"] = secrets.token_urlsafe(32)\n    56\t    return token\n    57\t\n    58\t\n    59\tasync def require_csrf(request: Request) -> None:\n    60\t    \"\"\"Dependency for every state-changing POST: the form's csrf_token must\n    61\t    match the session's.\"\"\"\n    62\t    form = await request.form()\n    63\t    submitted = form.get(\"csrf_token\")\n    64\t    expected = request.session.get(\"csrf_token\")\n    65\t    if not (isinstance(submitted, str) and expected\n    66\t            and secrets.compare_digest(submitted, expected)):\n    67\t        raise HTTPException(status_code=403, detail=\"Invalid or missing CSRF token.\")\n=== app/users.py\n     1\t\"\"\"User accounts: creation, lookup, and password checks.\n     2\t\n     3\tRoles are stored as `admin` / `editor` and shown as Director / Analyst.\n     4\tPasswords only ever exist here as argon2 hashes.\n     5\t\"\"\"\n     6\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n     7\t\n     8\timport re\n     9\timport sqlite3\n    10\t\n    11\tfrom argon2 import PasswordHasher\n    12\tfrom argon2.exceptions import InvalidHashError, VerificationError\n    13\t\n    14\tfrom app import db\n    15\t\n    16\tROLE_LABELS = {\"admin\": \"Director\", \"editor\": \"Analyst\"}\n    17\tMIN_PASSWORD_LENGTH = 8\n    18\t_EMAIL_PATTERN = re.compile(r\"[^@\\s]+@[^@\\s]+\\.[^@\\s]+\")\n    19\t\n    20\t_hasher = PasswordHasher()\n    21\t# Verified against when the email is unknown, so a miss costs the same time as\n    22\t# a wrong password and response timing does not reveal which accounts exist.\n    23\t_DUMMY_HASH = _hasher.hash(\"not-a-real-password\")\n    24\t\n    25\t\n    26\tclass UserError(ValueError):\n    27\t    \"\"\"A user-management request broke a rule; the message is safe to show.\"\"\"\n    28\t\n    29\t\n    30\tclass DuplicateEmail(UserError):\n    31\t    pass\n    32\t\n    33\t\n    34\tdef _check_role(role: str) -> None:\n    35\t    if role not in ROLE_LABELS:\n    36\t        raise UserError(f\"Choose a role: {' or '.join(ROLE_LABELS.values())}.\")\n    37\t\n    38\t\n    39\tdef create_user(email: str, password: str, role: str, name: str | None = None) -> int:\n    40\t    \"\"\"Create an active user. `name` defaults to the email's local part.\"\"\"\n    41\t    email = email.strip()\n    42\t    if not _EMAIL_PATTERN.fullmatch(email):\n    43\t        raise UserError(\"Enter a valid email address.\")\n    44\t    if name is not None and not name.strip():\n    45\t        raise UserError(\"Enter a name.\")\n    46\t    if len(password) < MIN_PASSWORD_LENGTH:\n    47\t        raise UserError(f\"Passwords must be at least {MIN_PASSWORD_LENGTH} characters.\")\n    48\t    _check_role(role)\n    49\t    try:\n    50\t        with db.connect() as conn:\n    51\t            cursor = conn.execute(\n    52\t                \"INSERT INTO users (email, name, role, password_hash) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?)\",\n    53\t                (email, (name or email.split(\"@\")[0]).strip(), role,\n    54\t                 _hasher.hash(password)),\n    55\t            )\n    56\t    except sqlite3.IntegrityError:\n    57\t        raise DuplicateEmail(f\"{email} already has an account.\") from None\n    58\t    return cursor.lastrowid\n    59\t\n    60\t\n    61\tdef set_role(user_id: int, role: str) -> bool:\n    62\t    \"\"\"Change a user's role. False if there is no such user. Takes effect on the\n    63\t    user's next request, since sessions re-load the user every time.\"\"\"\n    64\t    _check_role(role)\n    65\t    with db.connect() as conn:\n    66\t        return conn.execute(\"UPDATE users SET role = ? WHERE id = ?\",\n    67\t                            (role, user_id)).rowcount == 1\n    68\t\n    69\t\n    70\tdef deactivate(user_id: int) -> bool:\n    71\t    \"\"\"Block a user from logging in and end their open sessions. The row is\n    72\t    kept so their content stays attributed. False if there is no such user.\"\"\"\n    73\t    with db.connect() as conn:\n    74\t        return conn.execute(\"UPDATE users SET is_active = 0 WHERE id = ?\",\n    75\t                            (user_id,)).rowcount == 1\n    76\t\n    77\t\n    78\tdef list_users() -> list[sqlite3.Row]:\n    79\t    with db.connect() as conn:\n    80\t        return conn.execute(\n    81\t            \"SELECT id, email, name, role, is_active FROM users\"\n    82\t            \" ORDER BY is_active DESC, name COLLATE NOCASE\"\n    83\t        ).fetchall()\n    84\t\n    85\t\n    86\tdef get_active_user(user_id: int) -> sqlite3.Row | None:\n    87\t    with db.connect() as conn:\n    88\t        return conn.execute(\n    89\t            \"SELECT * FROM users WHERE id = ? AND is_active = 1\", (user_id,)\n    90\t        ).fetchone()\n    91\t\n    92\t\n    93\tdef authenticate(email: str, password: str) -> sqlite3.Row | None:\n    94\t    \"\"\"Return the active user with these credentials, or None — never says why.\"\"\"\n    95\t    with db.connect() as conn:\n    96\t        user = conn.execute(\n    97\t            \"SELECT * FROM users WHERE email = ?\", (email.strip(),)\n    98\t        ).fetchone()\n    99\t    try:\n   100\t        _hasher.verify(user[\"password_hash\"] if user else _DUMMY_HASH, password)\n   101\t    except (VerificationError, InvalidHashError):\n   102\t        return None\n   103\t    if user is None or not user[\"is_active\"]:\n   104\t        return None\n   105\t    if _hasher.check_needs_rehash(user[\"password_hash\"]):\n   106\t        with db.connect() as conn:\n   107\t            conn.execute(\"UPDATE users SET password_hash = ? WHERE id = ?\",\n   108\t                         (_hasher.hash(password), user[\"id\"]))\n   109\t    return user\n=== app/main.py\n     1\t\"\"\"The FastAPI application.\n     2\t\n     3\tThe admin console lives under /admin (login required) and the public preview\n     4\tat /. Routes live in their own modules under app/routes/ and are included here.\n     5\tKeep this file small.\n     6\t\"\"\"\n     7\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n     8\t\n     9\timport sys\n    10\tfrom contextlib import asynccontextmanager\n    11\t\n    12\tfrom fastapi import Depends, FastAPI, HTTPException, Request\n    13\tfrom fastapi.responses import RedirectResponse\n    14\tfrom starlette.middleware.sessions import SessionMiddleware\n    15\t\n    16\tfrom app import db, settings\n    17\tfrom app.auth import LoginRequired, current_user\n    18\tfrom app.routes import admin, auth, users\n    19\tfrom app.templating import templates\n    20\t\n    21\tSESSION_MAX_AGE = 8 * 60 * 60  # one working day\n    22\tALL_METHODS = [\"GET\", \"HEAD\", \"POST\", \"PUT\", \"PATCH\", \"DELETE\", \"OPTIONS\"]\n    23\t\n    24\t\n    25\t@asynccontextmanager\n    26\tasync def lifespan(app: FastAPI):\n    27\t    # At server start, not import, so importing app.main never touches a database.\n    28\t    db.init_db()\n    29\t    if settings.SECRET_KEY_IS_PLACEHOLDER:\n    30\t        print(\"WARNING: CMS_SECRET_KEY is unset or the .env.example placeholder; \"\n    31\t              \"session cookies can be forged. Set a long random value in .env.\",\n    32\t              file=sys.stderr)\n    33\t    yield\n    34\t\n    35\t\n    36\tdef create_app() -> FastAPI:\n    37\t    app = FastAPI(title=\"IPHS 400 MP2 CMS\", lifespan=lifespan)\n    38\t    app.add_middleware(\n    39\t        SessionMiddleware, secret_key=settings.SECRET_KEY,\n    40\t        session_cookie=\"cms_session\", max_age=SESSION_MAX_AGE, same_site=\"lax\",\n    41\t    )\n    42\t\n    43\t    @app.exception_handler(LoginRequired)\n    44\t    def redirect_to_login(request: Request, exc: LoginRequired):\n    45\t        return RedirectResponse(\"/login\", status_code=303)\n    46\t\n    47\t    @app.get(\"/\")\n    48\t    def public_home(request: Request):\n    49\t        return templates.TemplateResponse(\n    50\t            request, \"public/home.html\",\n    51\t            {\"title\": settings.SITE_TITLE, \"items\": []},\n    52\t        )\n    53\t\n    54\t    app.include_router(auth.router)\n    55\t    app.include_router(admin.router)\n    56\t    app.include_router(users.router)\n    57\t\n    58\t    # Must stay last: any /admin request no router above claimed, by path or by\n    59\t    # method. Anonymous visitors are sent to login (so they can't probe which\n    60\t    # admin paths exist); signed-in users get 405/404.\n    61\t    @app.api_route(\"/admin\", methods=ALL_METHODS,\n    62\t                   dependencies=[Depends(current_user)], include_in_schema=False)\n    63\t    def admin_method_not_allowed():\n    64\t        raise HTTPException(status_code=405)\n    65\t\n    66\t    @app.api_route(\"/admin/{rest:path}\", methods=ALL_METHODS,\n    67\t                   dependencies=[Depends(current_user)], include_in_schema=False)\n    68\t    def admin_not_found(rest: str):\n    69\t        if rest == \"\":\n    70\t            return RedirectResponse(\"/admin\", status_code=307)\n    71\t        raise HTTPException(status_code=404)\n    72\t\n    73\t    return app\n    74\t\n    75\t\n    76\tapp = create_app()\n=== app/templating.py\n     1\t\"\"\"The Jinja environment the admin console renders with.\"\"\"\n     2\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n     3\t\n     4\tfrom fastapi.templating import Jinja2Templates\n     5\t\n     6\tfrom app import settings\n     7\tfrom app.auth import csrf_token\n     8\tfrom app.users import ROLE_LABELS\n     9\t\n    10\ttemplates = Jinja2Templates(directory=str(settings.TEMPLATES))\n    11\ttemplates.env.globals[\"csrf_token\"] = csrf_token\n    12\ttemplates.env.filters[\"role_label\"] = ROLE_LABELS.get\n=== app/publish.py\n     1\t\"\"\"Render the public site into site/ as plain HTML.\n     2\t\n     3\tT00 publishes a placeholder home page. As you build content types, extend\n     4\trender_site() to write one file per published item. Two rules the rubric checks:\n     5\t\n     6\t  1. Only PUBLISHED content is written here. A draft that reaches site/ is a bug.\n     7\t  2. Every href and src is RELATIVE (\"style.css\", \"posts/x.html\"), never\n     8\t     root-absolute (\"/style.css\"), because Pages serves this from a subfolder.\n     9\t\"\"\"\n    10\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n    11\t\n    12\timport shutil\n    13\tfrom pathlib import Path\n    14\t\n    15\tfrom jinja2 import Environment, FileSystemLoader, select_autoescape\n    16\t\n    17\tfrom app import settings\n    18\t\n    19\tCSS = \"\"\"/* Minimal starter styles — make them yours. */\n    20\t:root { color-scheme: light dark; }\n    21\tbody { font: 16px/1.6 system-ui, sans-serif; margin: 0 auto; max-width: 42rem; padding: 1rem; }\n    22\theader a { font-weight: 700; text-decoration: none; }\n    23\tmain { margin-block: 2rem; }\n    24\t\"\"\"\n    25\t\n    26\t\n    27\tdef environment() -> Environment:\n    28\t    return Environment(\n    29\t        loader=FileSystemLoader(str(settings.TEMPLATES)),\n    30\t        autoescape=select_autoescape([\"html\"]),\n    31\t    )\n    32\t\n    33\t\n    34\tdef render_site(out: Path | None = None) -> Path:\n    35\t    out = out or settings.SITE\n    36\t    if out.exists():\n    37\t        shutil.rmtree(out)\n    38\t    out.mkdir(parents=True)\n    39\t    env = environment()\n    40\t    (out / \"style.css\").write_text(CSS)\n    41\t    (out / \"index.html\").write_text(\n    42\t        env.get_template(\"public/home.html\").render(\n    43\t            title=settings.SITE_TITLE, items=[], css_path=\"style.css\",\n    44\t            home_path=\"index.html\",\n    45\t        )\n    46\t    )\n    47\t    return out\n=== app/settings.py\n     1\t\"\"\"Configuration, read from the environment (never hard-code secrets).\"\"\"\n     2\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n     3\t\n     4\timport os\n     5\tfrom pathlib import Path\n     6\t\n     7\tROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]\n     8\t\n     9\t\n    10\tdef _load_dotenv(path: Path) -> None:\n    11\t    \"\"\"Read KEY=VALUE lines from .env into os.environ. Variables already set in\n    12\t    the real environment win.\"\"\"\n    13\t    if not path.is_file():\n    14\t        return\n    15\t    for line in path.read_text().splitlines():\n    16\t        key, sep, value = line.partition(\"=\")\n    17\t        key = key.strip()\n    18\t        if not sep or not key or key.startswith(\"#\"):\n    19\t            continue\n    20\t        os.environ.setdefault(key, value.strip().strip(\"'\\\"\"))\n    21\t\n    22\t\n    23\t_load_dotenv(ROOT / \".env\")\n    24\t\n    25\tTEMPLATES = ROOT / \"templates\"\n    26\tSITE = ROOT / \"site\"\n    27\t\n    28\tSECRET_KEY = os.environ.get(\"CMS_SECRET_KEY\", \"dev-only-not-for-production\")\n    29\tSECRET_KEY_IS_PLACEHOLDER = SECRET_KEY in (\n    30\t    \"dev-only-not-for-production\", \"change-me-to-a-long-random-string\")\n    31\t# A relative CMS_DATABASE is relative to the repo, not the current directory.\n    32\tDATABASE_PATH = ROOT / os.environ.get(\"CMS_DATABASE\", \"cms.db\")\n    33\tSITE_TITLE = os.environ.get(\"CMS_SITE_TITLE\", \"My CMS\")\n    34\t# Set this to your Pages URL once you deploy, e.g.\n    35\t# https://yourname.github.io/iphs400-mp2-cms/\n    36\tBASE_PATH = os.environ.get(\"CMS_BASE_PATH\", \"\")\n=== app/routes/admin.py\n     1\t\"\"\"The admin console. Every route on this router requires an active session:\n     2\tadd new admin routes here (or to a router built the same way) and they are\n     3\tprotected without further work.\"\"\"\n     4\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n     5\t\n     6\tfrom fastapi import APIRouter, Depends, Request\n     7\t\n     8\tfrom app.auth import current_user\n     9\tfrom app.templating import templates\n    10\t\n    11\trouter = APIRouter(prefix=\"/admin\", dependencies=[Depends(current_user)])\n    12\t\n    13\t\n    14\t@router.get(\"\")\n    15\tdef admin_home(request: Request, user=Depends(current_user)):\n    16\t    return templates.TemplateResponse(\n    17\t        request, \"admin/home.html\",\n    18\t        {\"title\": \"Admin\", \"home_path\": \"/admin\", \"user\": user},\n    19\t    )\n=== app/routes/users.py\n     1\t\"\"\"User management: Director only. HTTPException, Request\n     7\tfrom fastapi.responses import RedirectResponse\n     8\t\n     9\tfrom app import users\n    10\tfrom app.auth import require_csrf, require_director\n    11\tfrom app.templating import templates\n    12\t\n    13\trouter = APIRouter(prefix=\"/admin/users\", dependencies=[Depends(require_director)])\n    14\t\n    15\t\n    16\tdef _users_page(request: Request, user: sqlite3.Row, *, error: str | None = None,\n    17\t                form: dict | None = None, status_code: int = 200):\n    18\t    return templates.TemplateResponse(\n    19\t        request, \"admin/users.html\",\n    20\t        {\"title\": \"Users\", \"home_path\": \"/admin\", \"user\": user,\n    21\t         \"users\": users.list_users(), \"role_labels\": users.ROLE_LABELS,\n    22\t         \"min_password_length\": users.MIN_PASSWORD_LENGTH,\n    23\t         \"error\": error, \"form\": form or {}},\n    24\t        status_code=status_code,\n    25\t    )\n    26\t\n    27\t\n    28\t@router.get(\"\")\n    29\tdef list_users(request: Request, user=Depends(require_director)):\n    30\t    return _users_page(request, user)\n    31\t\n    32\t\n    33\t@router.post(\"\", dependencies=[Depends(require_csrf)])\n    34\tdef create_user(request: Request, user=Depends(require_director),\n    35\t                email: str = Form(\"\"), name: str = Form(\"\"),\n    36\t \"name\": name, \"role\": role})\n    43\t    return RedirectResponse(\"/admin/users\", status_code=303)\n    44\t\n    45\t\n    46\t# A Director may not demote or deactivate themselves: the acting Director always\n    47\t# stays an active Director, so the office can never lose its last one.\n    48\tSELF_LOCKOUT = \"You can't change the role of, or deactivate, your own account.\"\n    49\t\n    50\t\n    51\t@router.post(\"/{user_id}/role\", dependencies=[Depends(require_csrf)])\n    52\tdef change_role(request: Request, user_id: int, user=Depends(require_director),\n    53\t  raise HTTPException(status_code=404)\n    62\t    return RedirectResponse(\"/admin/users\", status_code=303)\n    63\t\n    64\t\n    65\t@router.post(\"/{user_id}/deactivate\", dependencies=[Depends(require_csrf)])\n    66\tdef deactivate(request: Request, user_id: int, user=Depends(require_director)):\n    67\t    if user_id == user[\"id\"]:\n    68\t        return _users_page(request, user, error=SELF_LOCKOUT, status_code=400)\n    69\t    if not users.deactivate(user_id):\n    70\t        raise HTTPException(status_code=404)\n    71\t    return RedirectResponse(\"/admin/users\", status_code=303)\n=== app/routes/auth.py\n     1\t\"\"\"Login and logout.\"\"\"\n     2\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n     3\t\n     4\tfrom fastapi import APIRouter, Depends, Form, Request\n     5\tfrom fastapi.responses import RedirectResponse\n     6\t\n     7\tfrom app import auth, users\n     8\tfrom app.templating import templates\n     9\t\n    10\trouter = APIRouter()\n    11\t\n    12\tLOGIN_FAILED = \"Invalid email or password.\"\n    13\t\n    14\t\n    15\tdef _login_page(request: Request, error: str | None = None, status_code: int = 200):\n    16\t    return templates.TemplateResponse(\n    17\t        request, \"login.html\",\n    18\t        {\"title\": \"Log in\", \"home_path\": \"/login\", \"error\": error},\n    19\t        status_code=status_code,\n    20\t    )\n    21\t\n    22\t\n    23\t@router.get(\"/login\")\n    24\tdef login_form(request: Request):\n    25\t    return _login_page(request)\n    26\t\n    27\t\n    28\t@router.post(\"/login\", dependencies=[Depends(auth.require_csrf)])\n    29\tdef login(request: Request, email: str = Form(\"\"), password: str = Form(\"\")):\n    30\t    user = users.authenticate(email, password)\n    31\t    if user is None:\n    32\t        # Same message whether the account is unknown, inactive, or the\n    33\t        # password is wrong.\n    34\t
